## D. Historical Lessons

- 2026-07-21: A strict official-FLA D192/L10 FutureSeed comparison rejects Linear Attention equation enumeration as the next scaling axis. Installed `flash-linear-attention==0.5.2` files match the pinned wheel byte-for-byte, backend dispatch is disabled, short conv is Triton, and actual CUDA backward nodes are `ChunkGatedDeltaRuleFunctionBackward`, `ChunkKDAFunctionBackward`, and `ChunkGDN2FunctionBackward`; FutureSeed enters only through official `Cache`. At the matched 500-step gate, GDN/KDA/GDN2 pre-fixed holes53 exact is tied at `0.0176`, while CE is `0.9981/1.0252/1.0101` and 46-50 exact is `0.8594/0.6504/0.7539`; all are zero exact at 51-64. GDN2 spends 16% more parameters and about 28% more allocated memory without hard closure, while KDA is slower. Lesson: a newer, more expressive recurrence is not automatically more compute-efficient. Keep official GDN and return to the proven independent-data/long-training scaling path; do not turn this into seed, LR, or three-way no-FS tables.
- 2026-07-16: P-SCALE-034 overturns the premature same-state ceiling diagnosis under the full-diversity regime. Keeping D224/L12/H14/D16 native FutureSeed GDN, loop5/all-loop CE, effective batch128, data, optimizer, and objective fixed, step8000->10000->12000 raises mixed loop5 exact `0.2715->0.3066->0.3730` and official 56-64 exact `0.1621->0.1855->0.2285`. Mixed loop1 remains `0.0234`; loops3/4/5 produce the gain, and hard cases move `31->4->0`, `30->9->0`, `15->7->1`, or `29->12->5` wrong cells. The model was still data/compute limited at roughly one-quarter of a dataset-equivalent draw budget. This does not justify blind endless training: continue unchanged to step16000, then apply the predeclared rule that two successive later gates without a `+0.01` new best stop this state formulation.
- 2026-07-15: P-SCALE-033 isolates FutureSeed under matched long training. At step4500, no-FS remains at CE `1.6372`, fixed holes53/60/64 exact all0, and blank `0.2755/0.2777/0.2766`; FutureSeed reaches CE `0.6415`, exact `0.1055/0.1172/0.1230`, and blank `0.6166/0.6332/0.6133`. Mixed FutureSeed exact rises `0.0234->0.1113` across loop1->5, while no-FS stays zero. The position-thirds diagnostic is mechanism-specific: no-FS remains `0.2085/0.2696/0.3518`, while FutureSeed is nearly flat at `0.6216/0.6193/0.6223`. The predeclared low-ROI rule fires, so stop no-FS at4500 and do not rescue it with seed/LR/loss/noise/width sweeps. This supports cheap future-context initialization and useful recurrent refinement, but not yet superiority over explicit bidirectional/noncausal processing; that quality-throughput-VRAM comparison is the next paper gate.
- 2026-07-15: Joint data-plus-width scaling does not rescue D256/L12. P-SCALE-032 uses the proven 3.83M independent-board corpus, matched curriculum, loop5 all-loop supervision, and 8000 steps. D256 nearly catches D224 at step7000 but finishes lower on holes53/60/64 (`0.2461/0.2656/0.2617` vs `0.2559/0.3066/0.2852`) and on official 51-55/56-64 (`0.4219/0.1562` vs `0.4473/0.1621`). This rejects ordinary width as the next scaling axis, not scaling itself: data diversity was strongly positive in P-SCALE-029. Loop computation remains real, moving mixed exact `0.0234->0.2539` and repairing tens of cells. Stop D288/D320; the next paper-critical evidence is a matched long no-FutureSeed D224 control, then compute-accuracy comparison rather than a parameter-count table.
- 2026-07-12: Supervised loop-count scaling saturates beyond loop5 for the current full-diversity D224/L12 FutureSeed GDN. P-SCALE-031 raises `MAX_LOOPS` from5 to8 and gives all eight loops equal CE supervision from the exact step8000 checkpoint. At step8200, holes53/60/64 loop5->8 exact gains are only `+0.0020/+0.0000/+0.0039`; mixed exact moves `0.2812->0.2871`, while official 51-55/56-64 regress versus P-SCALE-030. Visual hard failures freeze or oscillate after loop5. Lesson: recurrence is essential through loop4/5, but more unrolled passes cannot create missing state information. Stop loop-count tables and invest in one larger full-diversity backbone or more efficient generic recurrent state.
- 2026-07-11: Long-training D256/L12 does not produce a useful width-scaling crossover on official Sudoku. P-SCALE-028 extends the exact step1000 D256 checkpoint to step6000 with cyclic 51-55/51-64/56-64 data and no mechanism changes. Holes53 exact briefly edges D224 at step3000 (`0.0391` vs `0.0352`) but ends far behind at step6000 (`0.0820` vs `0.1680`), even though D256 blank accuracy is slightly higher (`0.6156` vs `0.6120`). Official `51-55` exact is `0.1504` vs D224 `0.2832`, while `56-64` exact only ties around `0.08`. Lesson: simple width spends more FLOPs learning local token accuracy without improving global-consistency efficiency. Do not run D288/D320 tables. The bitter-lesson next axis is more independent data/tokens on the compute-efficient D224 frontier or a genuinely more efficient generic recurrent state, with compute-optimal scaling curves rather than parameter count alone.
- 2026-07-11: Restoring raw FutureSeed state magnitude through a bounded learned RMS gain is not the missing hard-Sudoku mechanism. P-DIAG-027 proves the raw magnitude is board-dependent (`raw RMS sample std 0.0502`), but the learned gain barely follows it (`sample std 0.00310`) and mostly learns a global `0.988x` contraction. Full loop5 exact falls `0.2363 -> 0.2109`; official `51-55` falls `0.3555 -> 0.3418`, and `56-64` falls `0.1582 -> 0.1367`. Loop computation remains strong (`loop1 -> loop5 +0.1875`), so the negative result is specific: a one-dimensional confidence amplitude cannot carry the missing board-wide constraint structure. Do not sweep gain range, initialization, learning rate, or seed.
- 2026-07-07: Doubling effective batch from 128 to 256 is not the missing hard-range stabilizer for D224/L12 native FutureSeed GDN. P-DIAG-026 step12300 holes53/60/64 loop5 exact `0.1562/0.1660/0.1758` was worse than P-DIAG-025 neighboring `0.1875/0.2246/0.2012` and below P-DIAG-021 hard peaks, despite still showing loop gains around `+0.15`. Lesson: the hard exact cliff is not just noisy board-level gradients; next useful work should change state information/content or data scale, not batch-size tables.
- 2026-07-06: Learned loop-update gates are active but not the missing stability mechanism for D224/L12 native FutureSeed GDN. P-DIAG-025 resumed the strongest step12000 checkpoint and the gate moved from about `0.952` to `0.9845/0.9818`, while preserving strong loop gain. But final full exact/blank `0.2266/0.6166` trails P-DIAG-021 `0.2363/0.6287`, and official `56-64` regresses `0.1582 -> 0.1348`. Lesson: changing how much state is overwritten is too weak; do not sweep gate init/bias/temp. Future state work must change what information the loop stores or return to clean curriculum/compute scaling with checkpoint selection.
- 2026-07-04: Hidden-aggregate Gumbel noise as implemented in P-DIAG-023 is a negative structural boundary. The random aggregation collapsed to one-hot token selection (`entropy=0`, `max_weight=1`) and produced enormous perturbations (`norm≈61582`), leaving step500 holes53/60/64 loop5 exact all `0` and blank only `0.257/0.261/0.259`. Lesson: the user intuition that training noise should act through aggregation is plausible, but not via single-token gamble injected into every depth update. Do not sweep noise scale; if revisiting, make it smoother/bounded multi-token aggregation with nonzero entropy.
- 2026-07-05: Bounded soft-group hidden aggregation noise is mechanically healthy but not useful enough. P-DIAG-024 keeps entropy near `0.98`, caps perturbations at norm `2.0`, and trains stably to step1000, but final mixed loop5 exact/blank is only `0.0234/0.5252`, official `51-55` and `56-64` exact are still `0`, and same-step clean D224/L12 is not beaten. Lesson: noise is not the first-order missing piece for the Sudoku transition cliff; pause aggregation-noise knobs and put compute into proven clean scaling or a genuinely better recurrent state/update mechanism.
- 2026-07-04: Cyclic bridge-hard D224/L12 loop5 continuation improves the hardest Sudoku boundary but remains non-monotonic. P-DIAG-021 final official ranges are `46-50 1.0000/1.0000`, `51-55 0.3555/0.7312`, `56-64 0.1582/0.5647`, with strong loop compute (`loop1->5 exact +0.2129`). But holes60/64 checkpoint exact oscillates and stage CE can become tiny without stable full-board exact. Lesson: loop and FutureSeed are doing real computation; the next useful scaling is better recurrent state/capacity or stability, not another same-shape 15000-step repeat.
- 2026-07-04: D224/L16 naive depth scaling is a negative boundary. It fits GPU1 at about `63.7GB`, but reaches step1000 in about `3916s` and only gets loop5 exact/blank `0.0234/0.5192`; official `51-55` and `56-64` exact remain `0`. Lesson: simple extra same-family depth is not the missing global-consistency scale axis; do not run L14/L18/L20 or longer L16 from scratch. Next should use demonstrated data/curriculum slope or a more efficient recurrent state/update formulation.
- 2026-07-02: Mixed hard curriculum is a positive data-scaling axis after D224 step6000. A short 100-step continuation from step6000 on `51-64` reaches holes60 loop5 exact/blank `0.1973/0.6603`, beating the pure `56-64` tail step8000 `0.1836/0.6396`, while holes53 remains useful at `0.1641/0.6342`. Lesson: the failed hard-tail result was likely too narrow a distribution, not proof that harder data is low ROI. Continue with broader hard curriculum (`51-64` or staged mixed hard) before inventing more state-side gates.
- 2026-07-03: Longer mixed `51-64` confirms the data-scaling signal but also shows non-monotonic hard exact. Step6600 hits holes60 loop5 `0.2031/0.6666` and holes53 `0.1816/0.6468`; final step7000 has stronger holes53 `0.1914/0.6568` but holes60 exact eases to `0.1953`. Official `51-55` reaches `0.3105/0.7322`, while `56-64` remains `0.0859/0.5938`. Lesson: broader hard distribution is better than pure `56-64` tail, but same-shape long training alone is not enough for the hardest range; stop blind 9000/12000 repeats.
- 2026-07-02: Naive gated scratch work memory is active but harmful on the D224/L12 transition probe. Step300 scratch diagnostics are nonzero (`scratch_gate=0.1251`, `scratch_delta=0.4044`), yet holes53 loop5 exact/blank is only `0.0000/0.4013` versus fixed D224 step300 `0.0020/0.4704`, and holes60 loop5 is `0.0000/0.4043`. Lesson: the bottleneck is not solved by bolting a small per-cell scratch state onto loop context; do not sweep scratch bias/noise/gaussian. Future state-dynamics work needs a more integrated recurrent formulation or a data/curriculum axis with demonstrated slope.
- 2026-07-02: D256/L12 width scaling is trainable but low ROI under the current native FutureSeed GDN formulation. It fits GPU1 at microbatch32/grad_accum4 and reaches lower CE than D224 around step650, but step1000 board metrics do not improve: holes53 loop5 exact/blank `0.0176/0.5150` vs D224 `0.0176/0.5284`, holes60 loop5 `0.0215/0.5308`. Lesson: after D224/L12 opens the transition, simply making the token/channel path wider is not the next scaling axis; invest in a more efficient recurrent state/update formulation or curriculum/data scaling with demonstrated slope, not D256/D320 width tables.

- 2026-06-21: official EqR Maze token accuracy is dominated by non-PATH cells; path-aware metrics and visualizations are mandatory.
- 2026-06-21: a generic path-token-weighted objective opens official EqR Maze path recovery, but the failure shape becomes high-recall broad masks, not solved paths.
- 2026-06-21: FutureSeed is neutral on official EqR once path recovery is rewarded; treat EqR as a strong noncausal baseline/boundary, not as the backbone where FutureSeed should win.
- 2026-06-21: causal RWKV on official path-weight Maze also converges to the same broad-mask solution, so this proxy/objective does not isolate the missing-future-context mechanism.
- 2026-06-24: if FutureSeed is proposed as a cheap bidirectional mixer, the scan implementation is part of the claim. A Python loop over sequence positions is not acceptable: it stalled under `torch.compile` and was too slow without compile. The first viable path was vectorized/parallel-prefix scan; follow-up commit `d9c2600` added a Triton CUDA custom-autograd scan that matches prefix outputs/gradients and is about `12.2x` faster on the microcheck after warmup.
- 2026-06-21: adding a generic PATH/non-PATH boundary objective makes broad masks costly, but the failure changes to overpruning true path cells. FutureSeed mildly improves recall/F1 under this pressure, yet loop gain is still near zero; do not turn this into loss-weight sweeps.
- 2026-06-21: FutureSeed has a strong causal/RWKV Sudoku mechanism result, but official EqR Maze support is not established.
- 2026-06-21: no selector, repair, search, or maze-specific postprocessing should be used to rescue Maze scores.
- 2026-06-22: H96 official EqR compression gate is also broad-mask neutral: FutureSeed delta `-0.000178`, recall `1.0`, FP about `271`/case. Do not continue hidden-size/gate/seed sweeps; Maze needs generic recurrent denoising/attractor training or a different output objective.
- 2026-06-22: generic denoising-attractor feedback is also broad-mask neutral on official Maze30. no-FS and FS both hit the step300 kill rule with negative loop gain and FP increasing from loop1 to loop16. DAT teaches a fixed point for the existing wide mask, not late-loop correction; do not sweep corruption mix, DAT weight, or seeds.
- 2026-06-22: Maze abortable probes now preserve hard-case evidence. `rwkv_maze_probe.py` writes `abort.json`, partial metrics, README, and loop1/4/8/16 hard-case HTML before returning on broad-mask kill. The verification run also confirmed the failure shape: loop16 copied loop1 exactly at step100 with recall `1.0` and FP `272.1`/case.
- 2026-06-23: Pure Maze scale has a real but insufficient loop signal. D256/L12 train8/eval16 cannot run no-checkpoint on A800 80GB even at batch14. Activation-checkpoint b64 with lr1e-4 is stable and loop16 cuts about `44` FP/case with zero FN at step100-200, but the mask remains extremely broad (`pred_path_frac≈0.71`) and F1 plateaus around `0.314`. Scale alone is not the Maze fix under the current path-weight CE objective.
- 2026-06-23: Expanding RWKV recurrent state by increasing `head_dim` from 32 to 64 is a real capacity signal but a bad throughput axis. State64 b32 moved official Maze30 from the state32 broad-mask regime (`loop16 F1 0.3144`, pred_frac `0.7097`, FP `519.4`) to the EqR-like path-weight regime (`loop16 F1 0.4686`, pred_frac `0.4322`, FP `269.7`, FN `0`), but loop1 and loop16 were identical at step100/200. Lesson: recurrent state capacity can fix the operating point, but it still does not make later loops correct mistakes; future scale should prefer head_dim32 with more heads/width or a simple learned decision state, not head_dim64.
- 2026-06-23: Official EqR Maze-Unique released-checkpoint reproduction is now established: D16/B1 `0.827` vs paper `0.822`, D64/B1 `0.892` vs `0.889`, and D64/B128 convergence top-1 `0.944444` vs `0.930`. The key reproduction setting is Maze `noise_scale=0.01`; generic `0.5` destroys exact accuracy while token accuracy remains misleadingly high.
- 2026-06-23: Official EqR Sudoku-Extreme quick gate is now reproduced on GPU1: seed0 D64/B128/noise0.5/top4 gives top1 `0.991699`, top4 exact `0.986816`, majority exact `0.987305`, any-correct `0.992676` over 2048 examples, matching the README quick target. Full paper-scale Sudoku remains a separate 8-GPU 423,168-example job; do not claim it was run.
- 2026-06-23: The old official EqR FutureSeed patch is not a strong cheap-bidirectional test by itself: it injects H/L latent state into the other level, but under causal attention it does not create an independent future-token source. Its neutral Maze causal/compression results should be interpreted as a boundary for that add-on, not a verdict on the broader FutureSeed-as-cheap-bidirectional mechanism.
- 2026-06-24: Stop low-ROI baseline seed completion once the next mechanism decision is supported. Official Maze paper-point reproduction plus Sudoku quick seed0/1/2 in-range is enough to move to FutureSeed; continuing seeds 3/4 would only fill a table and delay the actual hypothesis test.
- 2026-06-24: Cheap-bidirectional FutureSeed on official EqR Sudoku gives a real state-dynamics signal but not a score win at tiny budget. Under `arch.mlp_t=false` causal attention, reverse-causal FutureSeed lowers `residual_of_16_steps` from `167.90` to `82.85` and raises token accuracy by `+0.00649`, while exact remains `0` and eval loss is slightly worse. The next valid test is one longer matched-compute scale gate; more seeds at this budget are low ROI.
- 2026-06-24: The longer cheap-bidirectional scale gate strengthens the mechanism boundary. FutureSeed's early residual advantage reproduces at step250 but shrinks sharply by step500 (`-84.42` residual16 delta to `-9.80`), exact stays `0`, and loss is not better. This is evidence that the current FutureSeed injection moves recurrent state early but does not by itself create better decisions. Stop more epochs/seeds for this injection; inspect residual-to-exact mismatch or change generic state/readout dynamics.
- 2026-06-24: Correction: the official EqR mixer-replacement experiment was an off-mainline bidirectional scan mixer, not FutureSeed. FutureSeed means cross-layer terminal-state seeding. Do not rename a right-to-left scan as FutureSeed. The scan mixer had early sample-efficiency but failed the e1024 gate (`base acc=0.6644/exact=0.0249`, scan acc=0.4231/exact=0`) and is archived as a side negative result only.
- 2026-06-24: Native FutureSeed-RWKV terminal-state seeding is integrated into official EqR and runs through the CUDA statepassing path, but the first fair no-FS vs FS Sudoku gate is neutral/negative. At h192/head6/e64, no-FS vs native-FS gives eval acc `0.1651596` vs `0.1651476`, exact both `0`, lm loss `2.370409` vs `2.369608`, residual16 `222.3120` vs `222.3994`, and FS eval is `1.67x` slower. Lesson: the corrected FutureSeed definition is trainable but not yet a strong EqR mixer replacement; do not sweep seed/gate, change the generic state dynamics if continuing.
- 2026-06-25: Full standalone FutureSeed-RWKV-loop backbone is a different result from the failed 2-layer official EqR mixer transplant. On official EqR Sudoku arrays at D192/L10/loop5/batch128/600 steps, no-FS plateaued at CE `1.6486`, exact `0`, blank_acc `0.2618`; FS reached CE `1.0632`, exact `0.0137`, blank_acc `0.4972`, and loop1->loop5 exact `0.0015->0.0137`. Lesson: FutureSeed provides real optimization/opening value for the original backbone under official data; next scale this clean path, and fix `FULL_ROLLOUT_KS=` so empty means no rollout rather than default rollout metrics.
- 2026-06-25: Matched 1500-step official Sudoku scale strengthens the FutureSeed mechanism but bounds same-config epoch scaling. FS reaches loop5 exact `0.0293`, blank_acc `0.5291`, and loop1->loop5 exact gain `+0.0278`; matched no-FS remains exact `0`, blank_acc `0.2697`, and CE `1.6312`. Lesson: FutureSeed is the optimization/opening source in the full backbone, but more epochs at D192/L10 are not enough for strong Sudoku. Next ROI is capacity/data/curriculum or a simple generic FutureSeed state-dynamics change, not another same-size long run.
- 2026-06-25: Native GDN/FLA FutureSeed plumbing is implemented, but the scalable path is not ready. FLA `chunk_gated_delta_rule` forward/final-state works on CUDA, while backward stalls under the current setup; FLA `naive_recurrent_gated_delta_rule` trains but is too slow for scale. The matched generated-Sudoku small gate is neutral: no-FS loop2 blank_acc `0.2319`, FS loop2 `0.2383`, both exact `0`, with FS active (`fs_gate≈0.471`) but no clean gain. Lesson: do not scale naive recurrent GDN; make the fast GDN kernel/backward trainable before judging FutureSeed on GDN.
- 2026-06-25: A local FutureSeed-aware GDN Triton recurrent state path now exists. It preserves the native `(B,H,V,K)` terminal-state seeding layout and matches the PyTorch recurrence in forward, final state, and gradients. This is a correct/trainable first kernel path, but backward still uses PyTorch replay; do not claim final GDN scaling until the backward/fused training kernel is also Tritonized and matched no-FS vs FS gates run without the naive bottleneck.
- 2026-06-25: GDN Triton kernel tests must be shape- and loss-mode aware before writing the real backward. The current matrix passes `7/7` CUDA cases across no-h0/T1, state-only, non-power dims, strong decay, V64, longer T32/K32, and bf16 runner-like inputs. Harness lesson: treat unused reference gradients as zeros, and fail only when both abs and relative thresholds exceed tolerance; near-zero relative error alone is not a meaningful kernel mismatch.
- 2026-06-25: `GDN_MODE=triton_recurrent` now completes full train/eval/record on GPU1 from clean SHA, including a matched no-FS/FS 100-step generated 9x9 gate. FS is active (`fs_gate≈0.471`, `fs_state_norm≈7.54`) and has a tiny blank-accuracy edge, but exact is still 0 and the model is intentionally too small. Lesson: the path is runnable; the next bottleneck is a real Triton backward/fused training kernel and then scale, not more tiny gates.
- 2026-06-25: Real GDN Triton backward removes the replay bottleneck. Clean SHA `645011a` matrix passes `7/7` with max grad abs diff `3.81e-06`; the same small FutureSeed train gate drops from `69.10s` under PyTorch replay backward to `10.05s` with Triton backward. Lesson: do not keep validating tiny gates; scale GDN no-FS vs native-FS or optimize saved-state memory if that becomes the next bottleneck.
- 2026-06-26: Native FutureSeed transfers to the GDN backbone on official Sudoku. At D128/L6/loop4/600 steps, no-FS stays near the plateau (`CE=1.6355`, loop4 exact `0`, blank_acc `0.2686`), while clean FS opens (`CE=1.0667`, loop4 exact `0.0107`, blank_acc `0.4925`) with only `+5.6%` train time and about `+186MB` peak memory. Lesson: the mechanism is not RWKV-only; next work should scale model/steps or improve generic FutureSeed state dynamics, not run more tiny gates.
- 2026-06-26: D192/L10 GDN confirms FutureSeed is the opening mechanism, not just added capacity. Same official Sudoku budget, batch128, 600 steps, eval_n 2048: no-FS remains plateaued (`CE=1.6369`, loop5 exact `0`, blank_acc `0.2674`), while FS opens (`CE=1.0479`, loop5 exact `0.0176`, blank_acc `0.5021`) and has loop gain (`exact +0.0142`, blank_acc `+0.0418`). Lesson: for GDN, scaling capacity without FutureSeed is insufficient at this budget; the next ROI is longer/larger FS scaling slope, not more no-FS repeats.
- 2026-06-26: D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed has a real 600->1500 step scaling slope. Loop5 exact improves `0.0176->0.0293`, blank_acc `0.5021->0.5337`, and loop1->loop5 gain grows to exact `+0.0239` / blank_acc `+0.0790`. It matches historical RWKV D192/L10 1500-step exact and slightly beats blank_acc. Lesson: GDN+FS is now a serious backbone branch; next high-ROI axis is capacity/memory scaling, not another no-FS or tiny gate.
- 2026-06-26: D256/L10 GDN+FutureSeed is a negative simple-width boundary. It runs on GPU1 at microbatch64/grad_accum2, but D256 does not beat D192 at the same effective batch and 1500 steps: loop5 exact `0.0288` vs `0.0293`, blank_acc `0.5300` vs `0.5337`, CE `0.9991` vs `0.9875`, and runtime `2104.5s`. Lesson: stop blind width/D320 table filling; use data/curriculum or generic FutureSeed/loop state dynamics if continuing.
- 2026-06-27: D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed `loop_residual` is a negative state-memory boundary. Damped cross-loop FutureSeed memory is active and raises blank_acc slightly (`0.5344` vs fixed `0.5337`), but exact remains tied at `0.0293`, CE is not better (`0.9901` vs `0.9875`), and exact saturates after loop2. Lesson: the bottleneck is not just persistence of FutureSeed state across loops; do not sweep decay/gates. Continue with more data/curriculum or larger clean backbone training, or use a truly generic global-consistency objective.
- 2026-06-27: Clean D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed 3000-step long training is a same-config plateau boundary. It improves CE and blank accuracy over 1500 (`CE 0.9875->0.9633`, blank_acc `0.5337->0.5418`) but exact barely moves (`0.0293->0.0298`). Lesson: there is still soft learning, but global exact is not unlocked by more steps alone. Do not run 4500/6000 D192 repeats; move to larger clean backbone or more/harder data/curriculum.
- 2026-06-29: Exact-margin loss is a negative objective boundary. A per-sample weakest blank-cell true-vs-best-other soft-min margin objective is trainable (`softmin -0.967->-0.129`) but slightly worsens clean D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed 3000-step results (`loop5 exact 0.0298->0.0293`, blank_acc `0.5418->0.5362`, CE `0.9633->0.9902`). Lesson: the plateau is not fixed by another indirect loss on weakest cells; do not sweep margin weight/tau/target. Next high-ROI axis must be larger/generic state capacity or state dynamics.
- 2026-06-29: D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed loop8 dynamics is a negative loop-depth boundary. Loop1->3 does real work (`exact 0.0083->0.0293`, blank_acc `0.4651->0.5215`), but loop3/5/8 exact is flat (`0.0293`) and hard official cases stop changing after loop5. Lesson: do not burn GPU on MAX_LOOPS 12/16/24 with the same fixed state; the next bitter-lesson-friendly axis is more recurrent state/capacity or a simple generic state update that changes what later loops can remember.
- 2026-06-29: D192/L10 GDN+FutureSeed feedback-attractor corruption is a negative mechanism boundary. The generic feedback channel is trainable (`fb_next 0.102->1.714`, corruption frac about `0.35`), but exact remains at `0.0293` and hard cases do not get reliable loop3->5 repair. Lesson: previous-prediction feedback plus random token corruption is not enough to create an EqR-like attractor; do not sweep corruption probability/mix/scale/seed. Next ROI must change generic recurrent state/update dynamics or use a stronger global-consistency training signal.
- 2026-06-29: GDN `expand_v=2.0` is a weak state-capacity signal but not an exact-solving fix. It improves CE and loop5 blank accuracy (`0.5364` vs D192 baseline `0.5337`) and hard cases keep moving through loop5, but final exact ties the plateau at `0.0293`. Lesson: recurrent state capacity matters, but simple value-state expansion alone is not enough; do not sweep `expand_v`. The next high-ROI direction is a generic loop-state/update rule that can turn better per-cell confidence into board-level consistency.
- 2026-06-30: Direct EqR loss audit shows the official training objective is simple token CE plus halt BCE; the important non-clean ingredient is latent Gaussian noise inside each recurrent reasoning update. Before adding more feedback/ranking/boundary hacks, test one clean EqR-style latent-noise run on the standalone GDN+FutureSeed backbone.
- 2026-06-30: EqR-style latent noise is not the plateau breaker. `NOISE_SCALE=0.03` feature-diff noise is too strong and collapses early exact; `NOISE_SCALE=0.01` trains normally but final loop5 exact remains `0.0293`, with hard cases still stalling after loop3. Noise is scale-sensitive, but it is not the missing mechanism by itself; do not run a noise-scale table.
- 2026-06-30: Loss-level alignment narrows the bottleneck. Official EqR's objective is simple token CE plus halt BCE, while FutureSeed/GDN's clean opening is strong (`D192 no-FS->FS` CE delta `-0.5890`, exact `+0.0176`, blank `+0.2347`). The failure after opening is exact-vs-blank conversion: CE/blank continue improving, but exact stays around `0.03` and loop3+ freezes. Stop noise/margin/feedback/gate/seed/same-config-long tables; next work must change state capacity or recurrent state formulation, with eval CE/state-delta diagnostics.
- 2026-06-30: GDN+native FutureSeed reproduces the RWKV official Sudoku blank-count cliff rather than disproving the mechanism. D192/L10/loop5 at 1000 steps solves `46-50` blanks (`exact 0.9863`, `blank 0.9993`) but has exact `0` on `51-55` and `56-64` despite soft blank gains. Lesson: GDN can use FutureSeed, and the shared bottleneck is global consistency past roughly 50 blanks. Do not answer this by no-FS/width/seed/loss tables; target the `51-55` transition with generic state/training dynamics.
- 2026-07-01: `GDN_EXPAND_V=4.0` is a real 51-55 transition signal but not the solution. It preserves `46-50` exact `1.0`, opens `51-55` from exact `0` to `0.0098`, raises `51-55` blank_acc to `0.6019`, and hard cases keep reducing wrong cells through loop5. But the strong `0.05` exact gate is missed and train CE remains high. Lesson: recurrent memory/state capacity is a real scaling axis; do not sweep `expand_v`, move to efficient larger state or a cleaner generic recurrent state formulation.
- 2026-07-01: Continuing the `GDN_EXPAND_V=4.0` transition run from step3000 to step4500 gives real but expensive slope. Holes53 checkpoint loop5 exact climbs `0.0303 -> 0.0615`, final mixed loop1->loop5 exact is `0.0088 -> 0.0537`, and official `51-55` range reaches `0.0459` exact / `0.6555` blank while `46-50` stays solved. Lesson: brutal scaling is allowed when it changes the answer, but the next high-ROI scale axis must increase effective recurrent/model capacity; do not spend the next lease on a same-shape 9000-step repeat.
- 2026-07-01: D224/L12 with `GDN_EXPAND_V=4.0`, activation checkpointing, and LR `0.0015` is too aggressive: it reaches NaN loss by step100 during the easy `46-50` stage. Lesson: scaling is still the right philosophy, but blind width+depth jumps need optimizer stability; do one conservative rescue or smaller capacity jump, not a width/depth/LR table.
- 2026-07-01: D192/L12 with `GDN_EXPAND_V=4.0` also reaches NaN at step100 when `ACTIVATION_CHECKPOINT=1`. Because stable D192/L10 expand_v4 runs did not use activation checkpointing, the next diagnosis should isolate checkpointing before blaming depth. Lesson: debugging scaling failures is still bitter-lesson-compatible when it removes an infrastructure bottleneck; do one no-checkpoint D192/L12 run with smaller microbatch, not LR sweeps.
- 2026-07-01: EqR-style every-loop CE is the fair default but not the 51-55 solution. D192/L12 no-checkpoint all-loop training made intermediate loops much better (`step300 loop1 CE 0.0332` vs final-loop `0.3720`; hard-stage step600 loop1/last CE `1.0194/1.0038` vs final-loop `1.4687/1.0316`), yet eval exact was flat across loops (`0.0283`) and `51-55` exact remained `0`. Lesson: do not sweep loop-loss variants; all-loop mostly moves the same operating point earlier. The bottleneck is still global decision/state update capacity.
- 2026-07-01: D224/L12 no-checkpoint with `GDN_EXPAND_V=4.0` is viable on one A800 80GB at microbatch32/grad_accum4. The short 300-step lease-bound gate stayed finite, used about 44.8GB, and reduced hard-stage CE from `1.4890` at step150 to `1.0738` at step300, with holes53 loop5 exact/blank `0.0020/0.4704`. Lesson: bigger-network scale is back on the table; the earlier D224/D192 L12 NaNs were likely activation-checkpoint/custom-backward infrastructure, not proof that width/depth scaling is dead. Continue with one long D224 resume, not LR/seed/loss tables.
- 2026-07-02: D224/L12 no-checkpoint 3000-step long scale is stable and has real loop refinement, but it does not beat the smaller D192/L10 4500 transition run. Holes53 loop5 exact/blank improves `0.0176/0.5284 -> 0.0195/0.5449 -> 0.0352/0.5750`, and final mixed loop1->loop5 exact improves `0.0215 -> 0.0410`; however final official `51-55` is only `0.0195/0.6441`, below D192/L10's `0.0459/0.6555`. Lesson: larger same-family capacity is not automatically better per compute. One more D224 continuation to 6000 is justified by slope and explicit long-run request, but if it misses the D192 gate, switch to cleaner recurrent state formulation rather than more same-shape brute force.
- 2026-07-02: D224/L12 56-64 hard-tail continuation is a same-shape tail boundary. Step8000 still has strong loop computation on hard holes (`holes60` exact `0.0195 -> 0.1836` from loop1 to loop5), but it did not improve over the step7000 holes60 readout (`0.1875/0.6624` vs step8000 `0.1836/0.6396`). Lesson: after D224/L12 opens `51-55`, simply feeding more `56-64` samples to the same state formulation is no longer the best use of GPU; the next bitter-lesson-compatible move is a more effective recurrent state/capacity formulation, not Sudoku repair/search/selector or another same-shape 9000/12000-step tail.
- 2026-07-02: D224/L12 H7/D32 state-geometry scaling is trainable but not worth the compute. It doubles per-layer GDN state cells and fits in 80GB, but uses about `71.5GB`, takes `3212s` to step1000, and lands on the same quality curve as cheaper H14/D16: holes53 loop5 exact/blank `0.0176/0.5263` vs H14/D16 step1000 `0.0176/0.5284`. Lesson: raw per-head state geometry is a bad scaling axis here; do not run head_dim/head_count tables. The next useful move must improve state efficiency or state update dynamics, not just make the recurrent matrix bigger.
- 2026-07-15: Matched D224/L12 full-diversity no-FutureSeed is still closed at step1000: CE `1.6580` vs matched FS `0.9971`; holes53/60/64 exact is all0 and blank `0.2657/0.2677/0.2671` vs FS exact `0.0176/0.0215/0.0215` and blank `0.5145/0.5254/0.5250`. no-FS loop1->5 is neutral, and visual hard cases keep almost all errors/conflicts. This is strong optimization/sample-efficiency evidence for FutureSeed, but delayed full-diversity scaling requires continuing the single predeclared arm to step3000 before making a final causal claim. Runner lesson: `sum(HOLE_STAGES)` is the global resume endpoint; it must equal the intended target step.
- 2026-07-15: P-SCALE-033 remains strongly separated at step3000. Matched no-FS CE is `1.6379` vs FutureSeed `0.7939`; holes53/60/64 no-FS loop5 exact is all0 with blank `0.2737/0.2737/0.2755`, versus FutureSeed exact `0.0273/0.0332/0.0430` and blank `0.5788/0.5885/0.5844`. no-FS loop1->5 is still neutral, and its early/middle/late sequence accuracy remains `0.2057/0.2685/0.3488`; FutureSeed is nearly flat at `0.5840/0.5815/0.5863`. Same-checkpoint official visualization shows FutureSeed cases changing `14 wrong -> 0` while no-FS cases stay around `35 -> 32 -> 32` with conflicts fixed. This directly supports cheap future context and at least a large opening/sample-efficiency gain, but honor the predeclared delayed-scaling rule and continue unchanged to the step4500 kill gate; do not rescue no-FS with a sweep.
- 2026-07-24: A strict one-seed carrier audit found an avoidable initialization confound in P-BASELINE-003. Resetting seed before each model left 11/77 shared tensors different because RWKV7/GDN/GDN2/KDA constructors consumed different random draws before ChannelMix and output-head initialization. The completed SHA `6ea7f8e` RWKV7/GDN arms remain valid implementation diagnostics but are not a clean ranking. The formal rerun now resets the shared shell from a separate fixed stream, resets post-init training RNG, and requires a GPU hash gate with zero differing shared tensors before any arm is accepted. Lesson: same seed is not automatically common random initialization across different architectures.
